This recovers the notion of a vertical submersion whenever the base spaces agree, as well as the usual notion of a submersion when the base spaces are just points.

Lemma C.13
.

If
\varphi\colon Y/S\to Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}
is a rel–
C^{\infty}
submersion and
\psi\colon Z/T\to Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}
is any rel–
C^{\infty}
map, then the fibre product

{\raisebox{2.04439pt}{$Y\times_{Y^{\prime}}Z$}\left/\raisebox{-2.04439pt}{$S\times_{S^{\prime}}T$}\right.}

is an object of
(\widetilde{C}^{\infty}/\cdot)
.

Proof.

Since
S\rightarrow S^{\prime}
is a submersion, we may form the fibre product
S\times_{S^{\prime}}T
. Then,
Y\times_{S}(S\times_{S^{\prime}}T)
admits a canonical rel–
C^{\infty}
structure over
S\times_{S^{\prime}}T
. The result now follows from the canonical homeomorphism

(Y\times_{S}(S\times_{S^{\prime}}T))\times_{(Y^{\prime}\times_{S^{\prime}}(S\times_{S^{\prime}}T))}(Z\times_{T}(S\times_{S^{\prime}}T))\ \cong\ Y\times_{Y^{\prime}}Z

and
[
57
, Lemma 5.7]
.
∎

Corollary C.14
.

If
\varphi\colon Y/S\to Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}
is a rel–
C^{\infty}
submersion, then

\varphi^{-1}(\{y^{\prime}\}):=(\widetilde{\varphi}^{-1}(\{y^{\prime}\}),\bar{\varphi}^{-1}(\{s^{\prime}\}))

is a rel–
C^{\infty}
submanifold of
Y/S
for any
y^{\prime}\in Y^{\prime}
with image
s^{\prime}\in S^{\prime}
. Furthermore,

T\varphi^{-1}(\{y^{\prime}\})=\ker(d\varphi)|_{\widetilde{\varphi}^{-1}(\{y^{\prime}\})}.

Definition C.15
.

If
\varphi\colon Y/S\to Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}
and
\psi\colon Z^{\prime}/T^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}
are transverse, we equip the fibre product
Z/T:=Y/S\times_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}/T^{\prime}
with the unique orientation so that the canonical isomorphism

T_{y}Y\oplus T_{z^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}\to T_{(y,z^{\prime})}Z\oplus T_{\varphi(y)}Y^{\prime}

has orientation sign
(-1)^{\dim(Z^{\prime})\dim(Y^{\prime})}
for any
(y,z^{\prime})\in Z
. We call it the
fibre product orientation
.

Remark C.16
.

This agrees with the convention of
[
53
]
.

We call a rel–
C^{\infty}
manifold
Y/S

Hausdorff, respectively paracompact
if
Y
and
S
are Hausdorff, respectively paracompact.

Lemma C.17
.

Suppose
Y\xrightarrow{p}S
is a paracompact Hausdorff rel–
C^{\infty}
manifold. Given any countable locally finite cover
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
there exists a rel–
C^{\infty}
partition of unity
\{\rho_{i}\}_{i\in I}
subordinate to
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
, i.e., for each
i\in I
,
\rho_{i}\colon Y/S\to[0,1]/*
is a rel–
C^{\infty}
map.

Proof.

Refining
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
, we may assume without loss of generality that each
U_{i}
is of the form
h_{i}:U_{i}\cong V_{i}\times B
, where
V_{i}
is the image of
U_{i}
in
S
and
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the open unit ball, so that
\{h_{i}^{-1}(V_{i}\times B_{\delta_{i}})\}_{i\in I}
is still an open cover of
Y
for some
0<\delta_{i}<1
. Using the paracompactness of
S
, we may refine further and assume that
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in I}
is a locally finite cover of (an open subset of)
S
as well. Let
\{\tau_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a smooth partition of unit subordinate to
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in I}
and fix for each
i\in I
a smooth compactly supported bump function
\chi_{i}\colon B\to[0,1]
, which is identically
1
on
B_{\delta_{i}}
. Then

\rho^{\prime}_{i}(y):=\begin{cases}\tau_{i}(p(y))\chi_{i}(\text{pr}_{2}h_{i}(y))\qquad&y\in U_{i}\\
0\quad&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

is a relatively smooth map
Y/S\to[0,1]
supported in
U_{i}
. By the assumptions on
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
, the function
a:=\sum\limits_{i\in I}{\rho_{i}^{\prime}}
is rel–
C^{\infty}
and positive everywhere. Thus,
\{\rho_{i}:=\frac{1}{a}\rho_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in I}
is a suitable partition of unity.
∎

C.2.
Rel–
C^{\infty}
orbifolds

In this subsection, we adapt the definitions of
[
55
]
to the relative setting. The main (technical) result is Lemma
C.25
, allowing us to go from a global Kuranishi chart with corners to a derived orbifold chart with corners.

Definition C.18
.

A
rel–
C^{\infty}
groupoid

\mathcal{Y}=[Y_{1}/S_{1}\rightrightarrows Y_{0}/S_{0}]
consists of a groupoid, where the object and morphism space are rel–
C^{\infty}
manifolds, the source and target map are rel–
C^{\infty}
submersions, and all other structure maps are relatively smooth.

Its
orbit space
is

|\mathcal{Y}|:=Y_{0}/\sim,

where
y\sim y^{\prime}
if there exists
\phi\in Y_{1}
with
s(\phi)=y
and
t(\phi)=y^{\prime}
.

Definition C.19
.

A
morphism of rel–
C^{\infty}
groupoids

\varphi\colon\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
consists of two rel–
C^{\infty}
maps
\varphi_{i}\colon Y_{i}/S_{i}\to Y_{i}^{\prime}/S_{i}^{\prime}
for
i=0,1
that intertwine the structure maps of the groupoids.

We will call a rel–
C^{\infty}
groupoid just groupoid from now on. The usual definitions carry over.

Definition C.20
.

A groupoid
\mathcal{Y}
is
étale
if source and target map are local diffeomorphisms. It is
proper
if
(s,t)\colon Y_{1}/S_{1}\to Y_{0}/S_{0}\times Y_{0}/S_{0}
is proper.

Definition C.21
.

We call
\varphi\colon\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
an
equivalence of rel–
C^{\infty}
groupoids
if
\varphi
is an equivalence of categories and
\varphi_{0}
is a submersion. We call
\mathcal{Y}
and
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}

equivalent
if there exists a sequence
\mathcal{Y}\xleftarrow{}\mathcal{Y}_{0}\to\dots\leftarrow\mathcal{Y}_{n}\to\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
of equivalences.

Following
[
53
]
, we call an equivalence
\varphi
a
refinement
if
\varphi_{0}
is a local diffeomorphism.

Remark C.22
.

We need somewhat more definitions as our orbifolds are naturally given as transformation groupoids, a perspective which also easier for some constructions, and we have to pass between the two models for orbifolds back and forth.

Example C.23
.

Suppose
G
is a compact Lie group acting by rel–
C^{\infty}
diffeomorphisms on
Y/S
, i.e., acting smoothly on
S
and continuously on
Y
so that
Y\to S
is equivariant and the action map
G\times Y/G\times S\to Y/S
is relatively smooth. Then

[(Y/S)/G]:=[G\times Y/S\rightrightarrows Y/S]

is a groupoid. If
G
is finite, it is étale and proper.

Example C.24
.

A disjoint union of étale proper Lie groupoids where the object spaces have the same dimension is an étale proper Lie groupoid.

The following lemma is standard and written here for completeness. It is used to combine the output of Theorem
A
with the results of
[
55
]
.

Lemma C.25
.

Suppose
G
is a compact Lie group acting by rel–
C^{\infty}
diffeomorphisms on
Y/S
so that
G_{y}
is finite for any
y\in Y
and
Y/G
is paracompact. Then
[(Y/S)/G]
is equivalent to an étale proper groupoid.

Note that we do not require the action of
G
on
S
to be almost free. This will not be the case in our main application. The proof is a generalisation of
[
50
, Example 2.6]
to the rel–
C^{\infty}
setting.

Proof.

Let
y\in Y
with image
s\in S
be arbitrary. By
[
27
, Lemma 5.9]
applied to a neighbourhood of
y\in Y
and
\Gamma=G_{s}
6
6

6

The cited result assumes that
\Gamma
is a finite group. However, the same argument works with any compact Lie group, replacing the sum by integration over the Haar measure.
we can find a smooth submanifold
W_{s}\subset S
and
T_{y}\subset Y
so that
T_{y}/W_{s}
is a rel–
C^{\infty}
manifold,
T_{y}
is
G_{y}
-invariant, and the action map

(C.2)

G\times_{G_{y}}T_{y}\to Y

is a homeomorphism onto an open neighbourhood
U_{y}
of
y
. The inclusion
T_{y}\hookrightarrow U_{y}
extends to an equivalence

[(T_{y}/W_{s})/G_{y}]\xrightarrow{\simeq}[(U_{y}/S)/G].

Let
Z\subset Y
be any countable subset so that
\{U_{y}/G\}_{y\in Z}
is an open locally finite cover of
Y/G
. Define

\widetilde{Y}_{0}:=\bigsqcup\limits_{y\in Z}T_{y}\qquad\widetilde{Y}_{1}:=\bigsqcup\limits_{(y,y^{\prime})\in Z^{2}}\{(g,x,x^{\prime})\in G\times T_{y}\times T_{y^{\prime}}\mid g\cdot x=x^{\prime}\}

and define
\widetilde{S}_{0}
and
\widetilde{S}_{1}
similarly with
W_{s}
instead of
T_{y}
. The structural maps are given by

s(y,y^{\prime},g,x,x^{\prime})=(y,x)\in T_{y}\qquad t(y,y^{\prime},g,x,x^{\prime})=(y^{\prime},x^{\prime})\in T_{y^{\prime}}

and analogously on the level of base spaces. Multiplication, unit element and inversion are defined in the obvious way. This defines an étale proper groupoid
\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}
. Using equivariant tubular neighbourhoods, one shows that
\widetilde{\mathcal{Y}}
is equivalent to the groupoid
\mathcal{U}
defined in the same way with the collection
\{U_{y}/S\}
instead of
\{T_{y}/W_{p(y)}\}
. Finally, the inclusions
U_{y}\hookrightarrow Y
define an equivalence
\Psi\colon\mathcal{U}\to[(Y/S)/G]
.
∎

Remark C.26
.

This lemma shows in particular that equivalence does not preserve the property of being étale.

Definition C.27
.

A
orbifold

(Y,\mathcal{Y})
consists of a topological space
Y
together with an étale proper Lie groupoid
\mathcal{Y}
so that
|\mathcal{Y}|
is homeomorphic to
Y
.

C.3.
Going from a rel–
C^{\infty}
to a smooth structure

In this subsection we explain how to use the smoothing theory of Lashof,
[
41
]
, to equip the ‘total space’ of a rel–
C^{\infty}
manifold
X/B
over a smooth manifold
B
with a smooth structure so that the structural map
X\to B
becomes smooth.

Definition C.28
.

Let
G
be a compact Lie group acting continuously by rel–
C^{\infty}
diffeomorphisms on the rel–
C^{\infty}
manifold
X/B
. A
relative
G
-smoothing
of
X\xrightarrow{\pi}B
is a
G
-equivariant rel–
C^{\infty}
diffeomorphism
\psi\colon X\to V
, where
V
is a smooth
G
-manifold with a smooth submersion
p\colon V\to B
and
p\circ\psi=\pi
.

Theorem C.29
.

Suppose
X/B
is a rel-
C^{\infty}
manifold equipped with a rel–
C^{\infty}
action by a compact Lie group
G
that acts smoothly on
B
. Then, given any compact
G
-invariant subset
K\subset X
, there exists an orthogonal
G
-representation
W
and a relative
G
-smoothing of
X^{\prime}\times W
, where
X^{\prime}
is an open invariant neighbourhood of
K
.

Moreover, if
f\colon X/B\to Y/*
is a rel–
C^{\infty}
submersion, we can find a relative
G
-smoothing with respect to which
f
is smooth.

Proof.

The proof strategy is similar to the one of
[
41
, Theorem 1.3]
. However, instead of embedding
X
into an orthogonal
G
-representation, we will embed it into
B\times V
for some orthogonal
G
-representation
V
. For this, we will use that a rel–
C^{\infty}
structure (in fact, rel–
C^{1}
suffices) yields a vertical tangent bundle
T^{v}X
and any choice of rel–
C^{\infty}

G
-invariant Riemannian metric on
T^{v}X
determines a
G
-equivariant rel–
C^{\infty}
embedding

(C.3)

\varphi\colon\Omega\subset T^{v}X\to X\times_{B}X

where
\Omega
is an open subset of the zero section,
\text{pr}_{1}\varphi(x,v)=x
,
\varphi(x,0)=(x,x)
and
\varphi
is relatively smooth over
B
.

Suppose first that there exists a vector bundle
E\to B
so that
T^{v}X\cong\pi^{*}E
. Let
W
be the orthogonal
G
-representation and
j\colon X^{\prime}/B\to B\times W/B
be the relatively smooth
G
-equivariant embedding of Proposition
C.30
. For the rest of the proof we will replace
X
by
X^{\prime}
without further mention. Since
\text{im}(j)
is a relatively smooth submanifold of
B\times W/B
, we can use a relative version of the tubular neighbourhood theorem and find an equivariant rel–
C^{\infty}
retraction
r\colon U\to X
, where
U
is an invariant open neighbourhood of
j(X)
. Define

(C.4)

\theta\colon X\times W\to B\times W:(x,w)\mapsto(\pi(x),j_{2}(x)+w),

where
j_{2}
is the composition of
j
with the projection to
W
. This map is clearly equivariant and relatively smooth. Since
\theta(x,0)\in\text{im}(j)
, we can find an invariant rel–
C^{\infty}
map
\delta\colon X\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}
so that
\theta(x,w)\in U
if
\lVert w\rVert<\delta(x)
. Let
W_{1}\subset W
be the open unit ball and define
\theta^{\prime}\colon X\times W_{1}\to U
by
\theta^{\prime}(x,w)=\theta(x,\delta(x)w)
. Choosing
\delta
sufficiently small, we can ensure that
(r\theta^{\prime}(x,y),x)\in\varphi(\Omega)
. Now, define

(C.5)

\psi\colon X\times W\to E\oplus W:(x,w)\mapsto\Bigl(\theta^{\prime}(x,w),\text{pr}_{2}\varphi^{-1}((r\theta^{\prime}(x,y),x))\Bigr).

This is rel–
C^{\infty}
and equivariant. It follows from the same argument as in
[
41
, Theorem 1.3]
that
\psi
is an open embedding. Since we can equip
E
with a smooth structure that makes the
G
-action smooth, we can pull back the smooth structure on
E\oplus W
along
\psi
. For the general case one can use the following relative version of
[
41
]
as loc. cit: If
\rho\colon E\to X
is a rel–
C^{\infty}
vector bundle, then a relative rel–
C^{\infty}
vector bundle reduction
\varphi\colon\Omega\subset T^{v}X\to X\times_{B}X
induces a relative rel–
C^{\infty}
vector bundle reduction
\widehat{\varphi}\colon\rho^{*}(E\oplus T^{v}X)\to E\times_{B}E
. The proof of this claim is verbatim the same as the one of
[
41
, Lemma 1.6]
.

The last assertion follows immediately from the former by noting that in this case
X/B\times Y
is a rel–
C^{\infty}
manifold due to Lemma
C.13
.
∎

Proposition C.30
.

Suppose
X/B
is a rel–
C^{\infty}
manifold with a relatively smooth
G
-action. Given any compact
G
-invariant subset
K\subset X
, there exists a
G
-representation
W
and an invariant neighbourhood
X^{\prime}
of
K\subset X
with a relatively smooth equivariant embedding
j\colon X^{\prime}\to B\times W
over
B
.

Proof.

Let
d
be the rel–
C^{\infty}
invariant fibrewise metric associated to a chosen Riemannian metric on
T^{v}X
. To construct
j
, suppose we have constructed for each orbit a
G
-equivariant rel–
C^{\infty}
embedding
f^{\prime}_{x}\colon B_{\epsilon}(G\cdot x)\to B\times W^{\prime}_{x}
for some orthogonal
G
-representation
W^{\prime}_{x}
. Choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, we may assume
f^{\prime}_{x}
has bounded image in
W^{\prime}_{x}
(with respect to some arbitrary
G
-invariant norm) since
G
is compact. Equip
\mathbb{R}
with the trivial
G
-representation and let
W_{x}=W^{\prime}_{x}\oplus\mathbb{R}
. Then, define

(C.6)

f_{x}(y)=\frac{1}{1+\lVert f^{\prime}_{x}(y)\rVert}(f^{\prime}_{x}(y),1).

This is clearly still a
G
-equivariant rel–
C^{\infty}
embedding. Note that it takes values in the unit sphere. Now, choose a finite set
Z\subset X
such that
\{B_{\epsilon}(G\cdot x)\mid x\in Z\}
covers
K
and let
\{\rho_{x}\}_{x\in Z}
be a
G
-invariant partition of unity subordinate to it. Given these data, we can define

(C.7)

j\colon X^{\prime}\to B\times\prod\limits_{x\in Z}{W_{x}}

by

(C.8)

j(y)=(\pi(y),(\rho_{x}(y)f_{x}(y))_{x\in Z}),

where
\pi\colon X\to B
is the structural map and we extend
\rho_{x}f_{x}
by zero to all of
X^{\prime}
. Then,
j
is manifestly relatively smooth and equivariant. Let us check that it is injective. If
j(y)=j(z)
, then, as
f_{x}
has image in the unit sphere,
\rho_{x}(y)=\rho_{x}(z)
. Thus,
f_{x}(y)=f_{x}(z)
for any
x\in Z
so that
\rho_{x}(y)>0
. As some such
x
must exist, we can use that
f_{x}
is an embedding to obtain that
y=z
. Closedness follows from the fact that each
f_{x}
is closed.

In order to construct
f:=f^{\prime}_{x}
, we use an inductive argument as in the proof of
[
44
, Theorem 6.1]
. The first step yields a global embedding; for the second step, where we ‘filter’ the fibre, we restrict to a neighbourhood of an orbit. Fix, thus,
b\in B
and define the
G
-invariant closed subsets

B_{\geq b}:=\{b^{\prime}\in B\mid[G_{b}]\subset[G_{b^{\prime}}]\}\qquad B_{>b}:=\{b^{\prime}\in B\mid[G_{b}]\subset[G_{b^{\prime}}]\}

and set
B_{b}:=B_{\geq b}\setminus B_{>b}
. Note that
B_{\geq b}
and
B_{>b}
are disjoint unions of smooth submanifolds. Then,
X_{*b}=X\times_{B}B_{*b}
is canonically a rel–
C^{\infty}
manifold over
B_{*b}
for
*\in\{=,\geq,>\}
. Suppose we are given
G
-equivariant rel–
C^{\infty}
embeddings

(C.9)

f_{=}\colon X_{=b}/B_{=b}\to B_{=b}\times V_{=}/B_{=b}\qquad f_{>}\colon X_{>b}/B_{>b}\to B_{>b}\times V_{>}/B_{>b}.

Set
V_{\geq}:=V_{=}\oplus V_{>}
. Let
\rho\colon B_{\geq b}\to[0,1]
be a smooth
G
-invariant function with
\rho^{-1}(\{0\})=B_{\geq b}
. By the equivariant Tietze extension theorem, we can extend
f_{>}
to a
G
-equivariant continuous function

\widetilde{f}_{>}\colon X_{\geq b}\to B_{\geq b}\times V_{>}.

Then, define
f_{\geq}\colon X_{\geq b}/B_{\geq b}\to B_{\geq b}\times V_{\geq}/B_{\geq b}
by

(C.10)

f_{\geq}(x)=\begin{cases}(\pi(x),\rho(\pi(x))f_{=}(x),(1+\rho(\pi(x)))\widetilde{f}_{>}(x))\qquad&x\in X_{=b}\\
(\pi(x),0,f_{>}(x))\quad&x\in X_{>b}.\end{cases}

Using induction on the longest sequence of stabilisers
[G_{b_{1}}]\subset[G_{b_{2}}]\dots\subset G
(in an invariant neighbourhood of the compact subset
K
), this shows that we can find a
G
-equivariant embedding
X/B\hookrightarrow B\times W/B
for some orthogonal
G
-representation
W
once we have constructed
f_{=}
as in (
C.9
) for each
b
. Fix
x_{0}\in X/B
with
b_{0}=\pi(x_{0})
and let
B_{0}:=B_{=b_{0}}
and
X_{0}=B_{\epsilon}(G\cdot x)\cap\pi^{-1}(B_{0})
. Then
X_{0}/B_{0}
is canonically relatively smooth. To construct
f:=f_{=}\colon X_{0}/B_{0}\to B_{0}\times V/B_{0}
we use the same inductive construction as before, now filtering
X_{0}
by the (conjugacy class of the) stabiliser. Replacing
X_{0}
by a neighbourhood of
G\cdot x_{0}
, we may assume
G_{y}\subset G_{x_{0}}
for any
y\in X_{0}
up to conjugacy. Given functions

(C.11)

f_{=}\colon X_{=x_{0}}/B_{0}\to B_{0}\times V_{=}/B_{0}\qquad f_{>}\colon X_{>x_{0}}/B_{0}\to B_{0}\times V_{>}/B_{0},

we can use Lemma
C.31
and the formula (
C.10
) to construct
f_{\geq}\colon X_{\geq x_{0}}\to B_{0}\times V_{=}\oplus V_{>}
using a relatively smooth function
\rho\colon X_{\geq x_{0}}\to[0,1]
instead with
\rho^{-1}(0)=X_{>x_{0}}
and so that all (relative) derivatives of
\rho
tend to zero locally uniformly near
X_{>x_{0}}
. Since
X_{>x_{0}}
is a (disjoint union of) smooth submanifold(s), this can be achieved by using a tubular neighbourhood as in Lemma
C.31
. It remains thus to construct an equivariant embedding

f\colon X_{=x_{0}}/B_{0}\to B_{0}\times V.

Recall for this, that we assumed
X_{0}
to be a neighbourhood of the orbit
G\cdot x_{0}
. Fix a
G_{x_{0}}
-equivariant chart
\varphi\colon U_{b}\times U^{v}\to X_{=x_{0}}
. Let
B^{\prime}\subset U_{b}
be a
G
-slice through
b_{0}
and
S\subset U^{v}
be a
G_{b_{0}}
-slice through
x_{0}
. The key observation is that

(C.12)

G\times_{G_{x_{0}}}(B^{\prime}\times S)\,\cong\,G\times_{G_{b_{0}}}(B^{\prime}\times(G_{b_{0}}\times_{G_{x_{0}}}S))

equivariantly, whence
B^{\prime}\times S
is a
G
-slice through
x\in X_{=x_{0}}
, this space is relatively smooth over
B
, and the map

G\times_{G_{x_{0}}}(B^{\prime}\times S)\to X_{=}:[g,(b,s)]\mapsto g\cdot\varphi(b,s)

is a relatively smooth diffeomorphism (over
B
). Choose a
smooth
embedding
j\colon S\hookrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
. By
[
44
, Lemma 5.2]
, we can pick an orthogonal
G
-representation
V
admitting
v\in V
with
G_{v}=G_{x_{0}}
. Then, define

(C.13)

\psi^{\prime}\colon G\times_{G_{x_{0}}}(B^{\prime}\times S)\to B\times V^{\oplus n}:[g,b,s]\mapsto(g\cdot b,(j_{k}(s)g\cdot v)_{1\leq k\leq n}).

Precomposing
\psi^{\prime}
with the rel–
C^{\infty}
diffeomorphism in (
C.12
) yields the desired embedding.
∎

Lemma C.31
.

Let
X/B
be a rel–
C^{\infty}
closed manifold and
A\subset X
a closed rel–
C^{\infty}
submanifold over
B
. Given a rel–
C^{\infty}
function
f\colon A/B\to B\times V/B
, where
V
is some Euclidean space, there exists a rel–
C^{\infty}
extension
\widetilde{f}\colon X/B\to B\times V/B
. The same is true in the equivariant setting.

Proof.

Fix a rel–
C^{\infty}
Riemannian metric on
X
and let
\epsilon\colon A\to[0,1]
be a sufficiently small cut-off function so that
U=\bigcup\limits_{x\in A}{B_{\epsilon(x)}(x)}
is a tubular neighbourhood of
A
. Let
r\colon U\to A
be the canonical retraction and fix a cut-off function
\rho\colon\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
with
\rho(0)=1
and
\rho(t)=0
for
t\geq\frac{1}{2}
. Then, define
d(y):=\frac{d(y,r(y))}{\epsilon(r(y))}
for
y\in U
and set

\widetilde{f}(y)=\begin{cases}(\pi(y),\rho(d(y))f_{V}(y))&y\in U\\
(\pi(y),0)\quad&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

This is clearly rel–
C^{\infty}
and we can choose the extension to be
G
-equivariant by choosing the metric to be
G
-invariant and picking invariant functions
\epsilon
and
\rho
.
∎

Definition C.32
.

Two relative
G
-smoothings
\psi_{0},\psi_{1}
of a rel–
C^{\infty}

G
-manifold
X/B
are
concordant
if there exists a relative
G
-smoothing
\widetilde{\psi}
of
X\times[0,1]/B\times[0,1]
with
\widetilde{\psi}|_{B\times\{i\}}=\psi_{i}
.

In
[
41
, Stable
G
-smoothing theory II]
, Lashof proves that isotopy classes of vector bundle lifts of the tangent microbundle correspond bijectively to sliced concordance classes of equivariant smoothings. In particular, two smoothings associated to the same vector bundle lift (as is the case for us) are shown to be sliced concordant. For our main application, Theorem
4.1
, we need the following stronger statement.

Lemma C.33
.

Suppose
X/B
is a rel–
C^{\infty}

G
-manifold. Given any relative
G
-equivariant diffeomorphism

\psi\colon\partial[0,1]^{k}\times X\to\partial[0,1]^{k}\times V

over
\partial[0,1]^{k}\times B
where
V
is a smooth
G
-manifold with a smooth equivariant submersion
V\to B
and the diffeomorphism is constructed as in Theorem
C.29
, there exists an orthogonal
G
-representation
W
and a ‘filling’

\widetilde{\psi}\colon[0,1]^{k}\times X\times W\to[0,1]^{k}\times V\times W

of
\psi\times\text{id}_{W}
, that is, a rel–
C^{\infty}
diffeomorphism over
[0,1]^{k}\times B
, which extends
\psi
.

Proof.

Reparametrise the interval to
[-1,1]
. We first observe the following. Suppose
j\colon\partial[-1,1]^{k}\times X\to\partial[-1,1]^{k}\times B\times W
is an equivariant rel–
C^{\infty}
embedding. Fix another equivariant embedding
i\colon X\to B\times W^{\prime}
and let
r\colon[-1,1]^{k}\setminus\{0\}\to\partial[-1,1]^{k}
be a smooth retraction. Pick a cut-off function
\chi
on
[-1,1]^{k}
, which is zero near the origin and
1
near
\partial[-1,1]^{k}
. Then, define

\widetilde{j}\colon[-1,1]^{k}\times X\to[-1,1]^{k}\times B\times W\times W^{\prime}

by

(C.14)

\widetilde{j}(t,x)=(t,\pi(x),\chi(t)j_{r(t)}(x),(1-\chi(t))i(x)).

This is clearly an equivariant rel–
C^{\infty}
embedding that extends
j\oplus 0
.
Going through the proof of Theorem
C.29
with the embedding
j\oplus 0
instead of the embedding
j
, we obtain the diffeomorphism
\psi\times\text{id}_{W^{\prime}}
instead of
\psi
. This completes the proof.
∎

Finally, we make the following observation, which has a similar conclusion to
[
3
, Lemma 4.5]
. For completeness, we include a proof.

Lemma C.34
.

Suppose
\mathcal{T}
and
X
are smooth manifolds and
f\colon\mathcal{T}\rightarrow X
is a continuous map. Let
\exp
be the exponential map of a Riemannian metric on
X
and let
\widetilde{f}=\exp_{f}\colon f^{*}TX\rightarrow X
be the stabilisation of
f
. If
X
is compact, there exists a smooth structure on a neighbourhood of the zero section of
f^{*}TX
such that

1)

\widetilde{f}
and the projection
f^{*}TX\rightarrow\mathcal{T}
are smooth;

2)

if
\mathcal{T}/\mathcal{B}
is a rel–
C^{\infty}
manifold and
f
is rel–
C^{\infty}
, then the rel–
C^{\infty}
structure induced by the smooth structure on
f^{*}TX
agrees with the canonical one;

3)

if
f
is already smooth near a set
C\subset\mathcal{T}
, then the smooth structure on
f^{*}TX
is the given one near
(f|_{C})^{*}TX
.

If
f
is invariant with respect to a smooth action by a compact Lie group
G
on
\mathcal{T}
, then we can choose the
G
-action to be smooth with respect to the new smooth structure and assume
\widetilde{f}
to be invariant as well.

Proof.

Let
\epsilon
be the injectivity radius of the metric and let
g
be a smooth deformation of
f
with
||f-g||_{C^{0}}<\epsilon/2
. Then
g^{*}TX
has a canonical smooth structure, and there exists an isomorphism of topological (or rel–
C^{\infty}
) vector bundles

\Phi\colon f^{*}TX\cong g^{*}TX.

For
u\in\mathcal{T}
, the images of the maps
\widetilde{f},\widetilde{g}\circ\Phi\colon B_{\epsilon}(0)\subset T_{f(u)}X\rightarrow X
intersect in an open neighbourhood of
f(u)
. Let
h:=\widetilde{f}^{-1}\circ\widetilde{g}\circ\Phi
wherever it is defined. Then, we have that
\widetilde{f}=\widetilde{g}\circ\Phi\circ h^{-1}
on a neighbourhood of the zero section. Define the smooth structure on
f^{*}TX
to be the one pulled back via the map
\Phi\circ h^{-1}
. The same procedure works in the
G
-equivariant setting.
∎

Remark C.35
.

Note that the zero section
\mathcal{T}\hookrightarrow f^{*}TX
might not be smooth with respect to the smooth structure on
f^{*}TX
given by this lemma, but it is rel–
C^{\infty}
whenever
f
is.

Appendix D
Extensions of Thom forms

The main result of this section says that we can extend certain differential forms given on a collection of submanifolds to a differential form on the whole manifold, if the restrictions to intersections agree.

Proposition D.1
.

Let
M
be a
G
-manifold with corners, where
G
is a compact Lie group acting almost freely, and suppose
E\xrightarrow{\pi}M
is an orientable smooth
G
-vector bundle. Let
M_{i}\subset M
, for
i\in\mathcal{I}
, be a finite collection of
G
-invariant submanifolds of
M
. Suppose that the intersection
\bigcap_{i\in I}M_{i}
is clean for all
I\subset\mathcal{I}
, and that we are given
G
-invariant Thom forms
\tau_{i}
for
E|_{M_{i}}
such that
\tau_{i}|_{M_{ij}}=\tau_{j}|_{M_{ij}}
for all
i,j
. Then there exists a
G
-invariant Thom form
\tau
of
E
so that
\tau|_{M_{i}}=\tau_{i}
for all
i\in\mathcal{I}
.

In order to prove this result, we introduce some definitions. Throughout, we will be working in the context and with the notation of Proposition
D.1
.

Definition D.2
.

Define the double complex

(D.1)

C^{p,q}(M_{i},E):=\prod\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset\mathcal{I},\\
|I|=q+1\end{subarray}}{\Omega_{cv}^{p}(E|_{M_{I}})}

Its two differentials are given by the deRham differential
d\colon C^{p,q}(M_{i},E)\rightarrow C^{p+1,q}(M_{i},E)
and the Čech differential
\delta\colon C^{p,q}(M_{i},E)\rightarrow C^{p,q+1}(M_{i},E)
given by

\delta(f)_{I}=\sum^{q}_{k=0}(-1)^{k}i_{I}f_{\hat{I}_{k}}

where
\hat{I}_{k}
is obtained from
I
by removing the
k
’th element, and
i_{I}\colon\Omega^{*}_{cv}(E|_{M_{\hat{I}_{k}}})\rightarrow\Omega^{*}_{cv}(E|_{M_{I}})
is induced by the inclusion map
M_{I}\hookrightarrow M_{\hat{I}_{k}}
.

Lemma D.3
.

The differential
d
is exact in degrees
p\leq\text{rank}(E)
.

Proof.

This follows directly from the Thom isomorphism theorem applied to
E|_{M_{I}}
.
∎

Unlike in the case of Čech cohomology (where one uses open covers), some more work is needed to show the following lemma.

Lemma D.4
.

The differential
\delta
is exact in all degrees, including
q=-1
, where
\delta
is just given by restricting from
M
to the
M_{i}
.

In order to show this, we first prove the result locally, generalising
[
40
, Lemma 2]
.

Lemma D.5
.

Suppose
M=\mathbb{R}^{n}\times Y
for some smooth manifold
Y
and that
H_{i}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
is a linear subspace (not necessarily of codimension 1) for each
i\in\mathcal{I}
. Assume
M_{i}=H_{i}\times Y
and let
E\rightarrow Y
be any vector bundle. Then the differential
\delta
is exact on
C^{*,*}(\{H_{i}\times Y\},\pi^{*}E)
, where
\pi\colon\mathbb{R}^{n}\times Y\rightarrow Y
is the projection.

Proof.

Let
f\in C^{*,q}(\{H_{i}\times Y\},\pi^{*}E)
be
\delta
-closed. Then for
J=(j_{1},\dots,j_{q})
, define

g_{J}=\sum_{I<k<j_{1}}(-1)^{|I|}\pi_{J}^{IkJ}\circ i_{IkJ}f_{kJ},

where
\pi_{J}^{IkJ}\colon\Omega^{*}_{cv}(E|_{M_{IkJ}})\rightarrow\Omega^{*}_{cv}(E|_{M_{J}})
is induced by the projection map
M_{J}\rightarrow M_{IkJ}
, and the sum is over all
k\notin J
and
I=(i_{1},\dots,i_{r})\subset\{1,\dots n\}\setminus(J\cup\{k\})
such that the tuple
(i_{1},\dots,i_{r},k,j_{1},\dots,j_{q})
is ordered. We allow for
I=\emptyset
. Then, for any
J=(j_{0},\dots,j_{q})
, we have

\displaystyle\delta(g)_{J}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{p}(-1)^{p}i_{J}g_{\hat{J}_{p}}

(D.2)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\neq 0\\
I<k<j_{0}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{p+|I|}\pi_{J}^{IkJ}\circ i_{IkJ}f_{k\hat{J}_{p}}+\sum_{I<k<j_{1}}(-1)^{|I|}\pi_{J}^{IkJ}\circ i_{IkJ}f_{k\hat{J}_{0}},

where we have separated the contributions from
p=0
and
p>0
, and used the fact that

i_{J}\circ\pi_{\hat{J}_{p}}^{Ik\hat{J}_{p}}\circ i_{Ik\hat{J}_{p}}=\pi_{J}^{IkJ}\circ i_{IkJ}.

We next separate the contributions with
p=0
further by taking terms

•

with
(k,I)=(j_{0},\emptyset)
,

•

with
k=j_{0}
and
|I|>1

•

with
k<j_{0}
,

•

and with
k>j_{0}
,

obtaining

(D.3)

\displaystyle\sum_{I<k<j_{1}}(-1)^{|I|}\pi_{J}^{IkJ}\circ i_{IkJ}f_{j\hat{J}_{0}}=f_{J}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\neq\emptyset\\
I<j_{0}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|I|}\pi_{J}^{IJ}\circ i_{IJ}f_{J}

(D.4)

\displaystyle+\sum_{I<k<j_{0}}(-1)^{|I|}\pi_{J}^{IkJ}\circ i_{IkJ}f_{j\hat{J}_{0}}

(D.5)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k>j_{0},I\\
(I,k,j_{1})\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|I|}\pi_{J}^{IkJ}\circ i_{IkJ}f_{k\hat{J}_{0}}.

First we show that the final contribution in line (
D.5
) vanishes, computing

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k>j_{0},I\\
I<k<j_{1}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|I|}\pi_{J}^{IkJ}\circ i_{IkJ}f_{k\hat{J}_{0}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k>j_{0},I\\
I<k<j_{1}\\
j_{0}\in I\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|I|}\pi_{J}^{IkJ}\circ i_{IkJ}f_{k\hat{J}_{0}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k>j_{0},I\\
I<k<j_{1}\\
j_{0}\notin I\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|I|}\pi_{J}^{IkJ}\circ i_{IkJ}f_{k\hat{J}_{0}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k>j_{0},I\\
I<k<j_{0}\\
j_{0}\notin I\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|I|+1}\pi_{J}^{IkJ}\circ i_{IkJ}f_{k\hat{J}_{0}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k>j_{0},I\\
I<k<j_{1}\\
j_{0}\notin I\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|I|}\pi_{J}^{IkJ}\circ i_{IkJ}f_{k\hat{J}_{0}}

\displaystyle=0.

The second equality holds as
\pi_{J}^{IkJ}\circ i_{IkJ}
does not depend on whether
j_{0}\in I
since in either case we set the variable
x_{j_{0}}=0
. Next, we note that

\displaystyle 0=\delta(f)_{kJ}=i_{kJ}f_{J}+\sum_{p=0}^{k}(-1)^{p+1}i_{kJ}f_{k\hat{J}_{p}}.

Applying
\pi_{J}^{IkJ}i_{IkJ}
to this equation and separating
p=0
and
p>0
yields

0=\pi_{J}^{IkJ}i_{IkJ}f_{J}-\pi_{J}^{IkJ}i_{IkJ}f_{k\hat{J}_{0}}+\sum_{p=1}^{k}(-1)^{p+1}\pi_{J}^{IkJ}i_{IkJ}f_{k\hat{J}_{p}}.

Next we rewrite the terms with
I\neq\emptyset
and
k=j_{0}
in line (
D.3
), that is,

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\neq\emptyset\\
I<j_{0}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|I|}\pi_{J}^{IJ}\circ i_{IJ}f_{J}=\sum_{I<k<j_{0}}(-1)^{|I|+1}\pi_{J}^{IkJ}\circ i_{IkJ}f_{J}.

We thus find that the terms with
p>0
in line (
D.2
), those with
p=0,I\neq\emptyset,k=j_{0}
in line (
D.3
), and those with
p=0,k<j_{0}
in line (
D.4
) are exactly covered by the relation
\delta(f)=0
.
∎

Remark D.6
.

We note that the same result holds if we work instead in a corner stratum
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{n_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{n_{2}}
and take appropriate coordinate subspaces.

Proof of Lemma
D.4
.

For each nonempty subset
I\subset\mathcal{I}
, pick a G-invariant tubular neighbourhood
\pi_{I}\colon U_{I}\rightarrow M^{\circ}_{I}
, where
M_{I}^{\circ}:=M_{I}\setminus\cup_{J\supsetneq I}M_{J}
. We may assume they are sufficiently small so that
\overline{U_{I}}\cap M_{J}=\emptyset
unless
J\subset I
. Moreover, as the intersections are clean, we can choose the
\pi_{I}
so that under the isomorphism
U_{I}\cong\mathbb{R}^{\text{codim}M_{I}}\times M_{I}
, the intersection
U_{I}\cap M_{J}
is mapped to
H_{J}\times M_{I}
for some coordinate subspace
H_{J}
. Here we have covered our
\overline{U_{I}}
by open sets so that the normal bundle to
M_{I}
is trivial over
U_{I}
. Let
\{\chi_{I}\}_{\emptyset\neq I\subset\mathcal{I}}
be a partition of unity subordinate to
\{U_{I}\}_{\emptyset\neq I\subset\mathcal{I}}
.

Suppose now
f\in C^{p,q}(\{M_{i}\},E)
is
\delta
-closed. Then, the image of
f
in
C^{p,q}(\{M_{i}\cap U_{I}\},E|_{U_{I}})
is still
\delta
-closed for each
I
. We thus obtain an element
f^{I}\in C^{p,q}(H_{i}\times M_{I},E|_{U_{I}})
. By Lemma
D.5
, we obtain an element
g^{I}\in C^{p,q}(\{H_{i}\times M_{I}\},E|_{U_{I}})
such that
\delta(g^{I})=f^{I}
. Setting
g=\sum_{I}\chi_{I}g_{I}
, we obtain that
\delta(g)=f
.
∎

Lemma D.7
.

In the situation of Proposition
D.1
, let
\tau^{\prime}\in A_{cv}^{*}(E)
be any
G
-invariant Thom form over
M
. Then, there exist G-invariant forms
\alpha_{i}\in A_{cv}^{*}(E|_{M_{i}})
such that
\tau_{i}=\tau^{\prime}|_{M_{i}}+d\alpha_{i}
and
\alpha_{i}|_{M_{ij}}=\alpha_{j}|_{M_{ij}}
.

Proof.

Let
k:=\text{rank}(E)
.
Both
\tau^{\prime}|_{M_{ij}}
and
\tau_{i}|_{M_{ij}}
are Thom forms for
E|_{M_{ij}}
for any
i,j
, so there exists
\alpha_{0}\in C^{k-1,0}(\{M_{i}\},E)
with
d\alpha_{0,i}=\tau_{i}-\tau^{\prime}|_{M_{i}}
. We want to modify
\alpha_{0}
to obtain
\alpha
with the same property satisfying additionally that
\delta(\alpha)=0
. As
\delta(\tau-\tau^{\prime})=0
, we find that
d\delta(\alpha_{0})=0
. By Lemma
D.3
, we can find
\sigma^{k-2,1}
with
d(\sigma^{k-2,1})=\delta(\alpha_{0})
.
Repeating this diagonally down the double complex, we obtain
\sigma^{k-2-i,1+i}\in C^{k-2-i,1+i}(\{M_{i}\},E)
with

(D.6)

d(\sigma^{k-2-i,1+i})=\delta(\sigma^{k-1-i,i}).

We now observe that

d\delta(\sigma^{0,k-1})=\delta d(\sigma^{0,k-1})=\delta^{2}(\sigma^{1,k-2})=0.

Thus, the elements of
\delta\sigma^{0,k-1}
are locally constant functions on
E_{M_{I}}
with compact vertical support. Hence, they must vanish. Thus, we can find
\beta^{0,k-2}\in C^{0,k-2}(\{M_{i}\},E)
with
\delta(\beta^{0,k-2})=\sigma^{0,k-1}
. Setting
\sigma^{1,k-2}_{1}:=\sigma^{1,k-2}-d\beta^{0,k-2}
we obtain a new form satisfying (
D.6
) as well as
\delta(\sigma^{1,k-2}_{1})=0
. Repeating this diagonally up the double complex shows that we can modify
\alpha_{0}
to obtain a cochain
\alpha\in C^{k-1,0}(\{M_{i}\},E)
with
\delta(\alpha)=0
and
\tau_{i}-\tau^{\prime}|_{M_{i}}=d\alpha_{i}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
D.1
.

Let
\tau^{\prime}\in A_{cv}^{*}(E)
be any
G
-invariant Thom form over
M
and choose forms
\alpha_{i}\in A_{cv}^{*}(E|_{M_{i}})
as in Lemma
D.7
. Then, Lemma
D.4
yields
\alpha\in A_{cv}^{*}(E)
extending all
\alpha_{i}
. Setting
\tau=\tau^{\prime}+d\alpha
proves the result.
∎
